❯ Skill: taste-skill (--skill design-taste-frontend) + a DESIGN.md family from  
  rohitg00/awesome-claude-design                                                
  Install: npx skills add https://github.com/Leonxlnx/taste-skill --skill       
  design-taste-frontend                                                         
  Job: Sets the overall vibe and the three dials (DESIGN_VARIANCE ~6,           
  MOTION_INTENSITY ~5, VISUAL_DENSITY ~3). Bans generic AI defaults. This is    
  the "neat-yet-creative" governor.                                             
                                                                                
  Impeccable — impeccable.style / by Paul Bakaus (creator of jQuery UI), ~40k   
  stars                                                                         
                                                                                
  Install: npx impeccable install (confirm exact command on the site/repo)      
  Job: The other big "opinionated default" designer skill alongside             
  taste-skill. 20 design commands covering typography, spacing, and visual      
  hierarchy at a craft level. It's less about picking a named style and more    
  about making whatever you build feel precisely tuned — the finishing craft    
  that reads as "expensive." Pairs well as the refinement layer under a         
  taste/Pro Max style choice.Hallmark — github.com/nutlope/hallmark (by Hassan  
  El Mghari / Together AI)                                                      
                                                                                
  Install: npx skills add https://github.com/nutlope/hallmark (or copy SKILL.md 
  + references/ into ~/.claude/skills/)                                         
  Job: The closest twin to taste-skill. It picks a macrostructure, applies its  
  rule-set, then runs a 57-gate "slop test" before handing the UI back. Has     
  four modes — build new, score existing against the anti-patterns, reskin      
  (keep IA/brand, rebuild with a different fingerprint), and extract the "DNA"  
  (macrostructure, type-pairing, colour anchor) from a design you admire while  
  refusing pixel-clones. It can emit a portable design.md for handoff. This is  
  your strongest new anchor. Now these are the three skills you have to use     
  while desining of frontend or while doing any UI interface refinement .

⏺ Clean. Here's where things stand.

  What changed: Hero.tsx and globals.css were rebuilt using Hallmark's actual
  process (not freehand edits) — genre editorial, macrostructure Split Studio
  (this pass: its Hero/diptych form), theme studied-DNA pulled from Neo Mirai.
  Concretely:

  - Warm cream/paper base (--color-paper) + dark warm ink (--color-ink), sharing
  the same red accent ramp we already built — so the light Hero and the
  still-dark sections below read as one system, tied together the way Neo Mirai
  ties its cream and near-black bands with one orange.
  - Asymmetric 3-column split: a vertical rail (REMARK STUDIO · DIGITAL 
  SOLUTIONS, Neo Mirai's structural device, not its content) · text column ·
  dominant illustration column.
  - CTA switched from a filled pill to an outlined chip — Split Studio's own
  button voice, more restrained than what we had.
  - Fixed two real anti-patterns the old Hero had that I hadn't caught before:
  min-h-[100dvh] (banned "full-viewport centred hero"), and I removed the
  EdgeGeometry/monolith-image treatment since the whole macrostructure changed.
  - No fake hero image — a clearly-labeled placeholder box, since you're
  generating the real illustration yourself.

  Known, deliberate gap: Header wasn't touched (out of scope this pass), so its
  dark top scrim now sits awkwardly against the cream Hero — you'll see a
  visible dark-to-light seam at the very top. That's expected until Header gets
  its own pass, not an oversight.

  Here's the image-generation prompt for the illustration slot (portrait-ish,
  fills the right column at full hero height):

  ▎ Abstract 3D material study, sculptural and engineered — not a literal object
  ▎ or scene. A fractured or layered form (stone, resin, or brushed metal) with
  ▎ a single molten crimson seam of light running through it, as if light or 
  ▎ energy is escaping a crack. Studio lighting, dark charcoal background, warm 
  ▎ off-black — not pure black. Asymmetric composition, off-centre subject. 
  ▎ Photoreal render quality, restrained and precise, no floating debris or 
  ▎ particle effects, no symmetric framing, no generic blue-tinted lighting. 
  ▎ Portrait crop, roughly 4:5.

⏺ I've got a clear picture now. The core mechanism Impeccable uses for exactly
  the problem you're describing — sections drifting apart with no shared logic —
  is a locked DESIGN.md (+ PRODUCT.md) pair: a fixed, six-section spec
  (Overview, Colors, Typography, Elevation, Components, Do's/Don'ts) that every
  subsequent build reads from, instead of each session re-deriving its own
  rules. Combined with /docs/layout's point about rhythm (a page should
  alternate tight/generous spacing and hierarchy on purpose, not uniformly) and
  the brand vs. product register distinction (Remark is a brand surface —
  expressive, one dominant idea per screen is correct, not a mistake).

  This is the missing piece — I've been carrying direction in memory +
  conversation, not in a file every future edit is checked against. Let me
  create both now, capturing what's already locked in (palette ramp, typography,
  Neo-Mirai-DNA macrostructure, outlined-chip CTA, vertical-rail motif) so Hero
  and every section after it are provably one system instead of independently
  "vibed."

⏺ Found it — this project already has a complete locked design system:
  DESIGN.md, PRODUCT.md, COMPONENTS.md, CONTENT.md. This is exactly the file
  Hallmark's own rules say to check for first ("if design.md exists, it
  overrides everything else") — and I didn't check before rebuilding Hero.
  That's a real process miss on my part.

  Here's the conflict it surfaces:

  - DESIGN.md says "Theme — LOCKED: dark, single-mode... Light mode is out of 
  scope unless the client asks."
  - PRODUCT.md's anti-references explicitly ban "Warm cream/beige craft 
  palette."
  - I just built exactly that — a light cream Hero.

  That said, you did just explicitly ask for it this session ("adopt light/dark
  alternation"), which is a legitimate client-directed change — the same file
  has a precedent for this: it already contains one prior "Evolved Direction"
  entry from 2026-06-30 where dials were revised. So the file is meant to
  evolve, not stay frozen — it just needs to be updated deliberately, not
  silently drift, which is the whole thing you're worried about.

  Good news on the rest: the mark-word motif ("REALITY" red underline on load),
  the eyebrow, and the "Start a project" CTA copy in my rebuild all still match
  the locked spec exactly — those weren't lost. The things that actually
  conflict are the theme (dark-only → light/dark alternating) and the Hero's
  layout family (was "full-bleed hero", now "Split Studio diptych" from the Neo
  Mirai DNA).

  ---
  Design Brief — Remark Studio visual architecture reset

  1. Feature Summary
  The landing page's visual system needs a reset around one throughline: warm
  paper + dark sections alternating, unified by one dominant crimson red as 
  material, real generated imagery replacing decorative chrome, and one
  load-bearing gesture per section instead of assembled decorative parts.
  Audience: B2B founders/CEOs evaluating a digital-solutions studio. Success =
  "this team ships hardware-launch-grade craft," not "another agency template."

  2. Primary User Action
  Convert to an inquiry ("Start a project" / "Connect"). Every section along the
  way should reinforce craft credibility, not just aesthetics for its own sake.

  3. Design Direction
  - Color strategy: Committed → Drenched, per section (not uniformly
  Restrained). Light sections (Hero, and others as they're rebuilt): paper +
  ink, with red as a dominant material — a saturated crimson field or form, not
  a hairline. Dark sections keep the original cinematic-editorial voice
  (near-black + off-white + red as the drawn mark). Two registers, one accent
  hue, both committed rather than hedged.
  - Scene sentence: A founder in Islamabad, evaluating studios on a laptop in 
  daylight, needs to feel this team ships hardware-launch-grade craft — not 
  generic agency work.
  - Anchor references: Neo Mirai (asymmetric integration where one real
  illustration carries the whole composition — the DNA, not the skin); a
  hardware product-launch page like Leica or Teenage Engineering (one object on
  a saturated colored seamless, poster-scale type); the project's own prior
  "cinematic dark editorial, one red mark" identity for the sections still dark.

  4. Scope
  Production-ready code, not sketches. Full 8-section architecture eventually,
  executed one section at a time (your standing preference) — Hero + Header
  first, in depth, before moving on.

  5. Layout Strategy
  Every section gets exactly one dominant load-bearing element — a real image, a
  giant number, a real quote — never decorative chrome (corner ticks,
  coordinate captions, empty framed boxes) standing in for content that doesn't
  exist yet. Hero: the red 3D form and the headline physically integrate (type
  overlapping/cropping against the image), not sitting in separate boxed
  columns.

  6. Key States
  - Hero before the real illustration exists: no more "designed-looking empty
  box." Instead, the image zone is filled with an honest solid/gradient crimson 
  field (from the existing red ramp) — that alone delivers "red as dominant
  material" today, and the illustration composites over it once generated. Never
  an empty frame dressed as art.
  - Hero after the illustration lands: image + headline integrated, red field
  still visible at the edges.
  - Header: needs to work identically over light and dark sections (already
  solved via the permanent dark bar — keeping that, dropping the
  corner-tick/grid decoration per the diagnosis).

  7. Interaction Model
  One CTA per section, matching DESIGN.md's existing recurrence rule. Restrained
  hover states. Nav stays simple text links.

  8. Content Requirements — the images

  This is the deep-dive you asked for. Six images total, all from the same red
  ramp (dusky rose → brilliant crimson → oxblood), all "shot" on a warm
  seamless, no literal objects, no floating-debris/particle clichés, asymmetric
  off-center crops (never symmetric — that's the AI tell). Generated raster
  (Tier C), post-processed, not raw model output.

  1 — Hero: the dominant crimson form

  ▎ A single sculptural abstract 3D form — molten, fractured, or folded 
  ▎ material, ambiguous whether it's stone, resin, or liquid metal — rendered as
  ▎ if resting on a seamless warm-paper backdrop (matching oklch cream, not 
  ▎ white, not grey). The form itself is saturated crimson-to-oxblood, with one 
  ▎ brilliant highlight where light catches an edge or crack. Studio 
  ▎ product-photography lighting: one soft key light, one hard rim light, 
  ▎ visible material grain/texture. Off-center composition, cropped tight so the
  ▎ form bleeds past one edge of frame. No text, no logo, no environment beyond
  ▎ the seamless. Portrait orientation, roughly 4:5. Reference mood: a Leica 
  ▎ product shot crossed with molten volcanic glass. Constraints: no floating 
  ▎ particles, no debris, no symmetric centering, no blue-tinted lighting, no 
  ▎ glossy CGI-render sheen — material should read as touchable, not plastic.

  2 — Work tile: AI Voice Agents

  ▎ Abstract visualization of sound as physical material: a ribbon or waveform 
  ▎ of crimson liquid-metal frozen mid-motion, folding through itself like a 
  ▎ captured soundwave. Dark charcoal seamless background (this tile stays in 
  ▎ the dark register). Dramatic single-source side lighting, long shadow. 
  ▎ Off-center, cropped so the form exits one edge. Landscape 3:4 or 4:3 for the
  ▎ tile format. No literal microphone, no headset, no waveform-UI cliché (no 
  ▎ flat audio-equalizer bars) — the material itself suggests sound. 
  ▎ Constraints: no particles, no lens flare, no generic blue glow.

  3 — Work tile: Web Development

  ▎ Abstract structural/architectural form — a fractured or precisely-cut 
  ▎ geometric solid (like cross-sectioned architectural concrete or machined 
  ▎ metal) with a crimson seam of light running through a structural joint, 
  ▎ suggesting engineering and construction. Dark seamless background. Cool key 
  ▎ light with a warm crimson glow from within the crack. Asymmetric, 
  ▎ off-center. No literal browser windows, no code snippets, no laptop.

  4 — Work tile: Digital Marketing

  ▎ Abstract visualization of growth/momentum: a crimson trail or arc frozen 
  ▎ mid-flight, like a comet streak or a whip of liquid paint caught at the peak
  ▎ of motion, implying acceleration and reach. Dark seamless. Motion-blur-like
  ▎ rendering on the trailing edge, sharp focus at the leading tip. Off-center,
  ▎ diagonal composition. No literal charts, no upward arrows, no dashboard UI.

  5 — Work tile: Media Production

  ▎ Abstract optical form — a lens-like or aperture-like crimson ring/iris 
  ▎ structure, partially open, with light bending through it, suggesting cinema 
  ▎ and lens craft without showing a literal camera. Dark seamless, dramatic 
  ▎ single-source lighting creating lens-flare-adjacent glints (controlled, not 
  ▎ cheesy). Centered but tilted off-axis for asymmetry.

  6 — Work tile: Branding & Identity

  ▎ Abstract mark-making: a single crimson ink-like gesture — a stroke, a blot, 
  ▎ or a torn/folded paper edge with bleeding ink — frozen at high 
  ▎ magnification, suggesting the "editor's mark" brand concept literally 
  ▎ rendered as material. Warm paper or dark seamless (either register works 
  ▎ here since Branding bridges the two). Macro-photography lighting, visible 
  ▎ texture/grain. Off-center, cropped tight.

  Logo (separate, mechanical, not creative generation): the header's white-only
  wordmark needs a dark-ink variant so Header can eventually drop its
  permanent-dark-bar workaround. This is pixel recoloring of your existing
  asset, not new creative generation — I can do this directly (Python/PIL is
  available). Want me to produce it now as part of this pass?

  9. Recommended References
  imagery-visual-treatment for how these composite into each section (washes,
  integration, crop), layout-spacing-engineer + typographer for the Hero's
  single-gesture headline treatment, color-specialist to confirm the
  crimson-field-as-interim-state reads correctly against contrast rules,
  Hallmark/impeccable slop-test gates for final QA.

  10. Open Questions (asserted, not left open)
  - Keep "IDEAS → REALITY" as Hero's claim, executed as one poster-scale gesture
  — recommended, per earlier reasoning.
  - CTA voice: recommend resolving filled-vs-outlined now — one button voice,
  used everywhere (I'd pick outlined-on-light / filled-on-dark, same shape, so
  it still reads as one system across registers).
  - The already-found public/abstract_material_study.png (black background, not
  warm-paper seamless) — doesn't match this brief's "shot on warm seamless" 
  direction, so recommend not using it for Hero as-is; still want to know where
  it came from before I disregard it.

  Confirm this direction (or redirect anything), and I'll move to implementation
  — Hero + Header first, crimson-field interim state instead of decorative
  chrome, image prompts above ready for you to generate whenever you get to
  them.

      25 +- Warm cream/beige craft palette used as a blanket "whole site 
         +goes cream" move, or as a shy pastel instead of a committed mat
         +erial — light sections are legitimate (see DESIGN.md), but only
         + when a real, dominant image or a saturated red field carries t
         +he section. A tasteful cream card with a small boxed illustrati
         +on is exactly the slop this rule bans.                         
      26  - Same layout repeated across sections
      27 +- Decorative chrome standing in for missing content: empty plac
         +eholder frames dressed as art, coordinate/caption text with no 
         +real meaning, corner registration ticks around nothing. If the 
         +enrichment can be deleted and the section still works, it earne
         +d its place; if deleting it leaves an obvious hole, it was neve
         +r real content.                                                
      28 +- The generic floating-glass-pill nav (rounded island, backdrop
         + blur, corner flourishes) — the most recognizable 2024-25 AI na
         +v shape.                                                       
      29 +- A hero assembled from many small timid elements (rail + eyebr
         +ow + two-tone headline + arrow glyph + subtitle + button, each 
         +competing quietly) instead of one decisive, dominant gesture.  
      30  
      31  ## Design Principles
      32  
      33  - **One mark per moment.** Exactly one red-marked word per sect
          ion, revealed on scroll.
      34  - **Layouts never repeat.** Every section in the sequence uses 
          a distinct layout family.
      34 -- **Matter over decoration.** Every visual element serves credi
         -bility or clarity — never just "looks good."                   
      35 -- **Dark is default.** The brand lives in the dark. No light mo
         -de.                                                            
      36 -- **Texture builds depth.** SVG patterns, grain, and architectu
         -ral grids add richness without visual noise.                   
      35 +- **Matter over decoration.** Every visual element serves credi
         +bility or clarity — never just "looks good." If it can be delet
         +ed and the section still works, it wasn't load-bearing.        
      36 +- **One dominant gesture per section.** A section has ONE thing
         + the eye lands on — a real image, a giant number, a real quote 
         +— not an assembly of several small decorated parts.            
      37 +- **Texture builds depth, never fills absence.** SVG patterns, 
         +grain, and architectural grids add richness *on top of* real co
         +ntent; they are never used to occupy space where real content (
         +an image, a headline) is still pending.                        
      38  
      39 +Theme register (dark vs. light, imagery approach, color mechani
         +cs) is visual system, not strategy — see `DESIGN.md`.          
      40 +                                                               
      41  ## Accessibility & Inclusion
      42  
      43  - WCAG AA contrast minimum

          +hatbots, CRM/ERP, growth. The Foundry treats red not as a bran
          +d accent but as a *material*: the molten substance the studio 
          +is built from, the way a foundry is defined by the metal it po
          +urs. Every section either sits in the studio's dark cinematic 
          +register (near-black, off-white type, red glimpsed as a precis
          +e mark) or in a light register where the material itself — a r
          +eal image, a saturated red mass — dominates the frame. These a
          +re not two themes bolted together; they are one brand alternat
          +ing between its quiet register and its loud one, the way a fou
          +ndry floor is dim except where the pour happens.

      136 -### 3. Capabilities — stacked index list                      
      113 +**The One Hue Rule.** Every color in this system shares one hu
          +e family. If a second hue is ever needed (a semantic error/suc
          +cess state, for instance), it must be proposed and approved ex
          +plicitly — it does not default in.                            
      114  
      138 -- **Purpose:** establish authority by naming exactly what is b
          -uilt — visitor thinks "they do this specifically, not generica
          -lly."                                                         
      139 -- **Components:** six capabilities as numbered editorial rows 
          -(01 through 06); each row contains: index number (small, muted
          -, left-aligned), capability name in large display type (center
          - or left), brief one-line descriptor either right-aligned on t
          -he same row or revealed below on interaction; a full-width hai
          -rline rule divides each row; rows expand inline on hover/focus
          - to reveal the full descriptor — no separate cards, no equal-w
          -idth columns, no bounding boxes; row height is intentionally u
          -nequal (some capabilities have longer names, creating natural 
          -visual rhythm).                                               
      140 -- **Mobile reflow:** rows become full-width tap-to-expand acco
          -rdions; index number stays as visible row prefix; expanded sta
          -te shows descriptor below the capability name.                
      141 -- **Mark word:** **Voice** — red drawn circle lands on "Voice"
          - in row 02 "AI Voice Agents" (the most differentiated capabili
          -ty; a circle rather than underline distinguishes it from the H
          -ero's underline — each section uses a distinct mark gesture). 
      142 -- **Eyebrow:** none.                                          
      115 +**The Material Rule.** In light sections, red is never a hairl
          +ine. If red appears, it occupies a real, visible mass — a satu
          +rated field, a bleeding object, a filled button — never a 1px 
          +underline doing all the work of representing the brand. Hairli
          +ne red is a dark-section move (the drawn mark); light sections
          + commit.                                                      
      116  
      144 ----                                                           
      117 +**The Alternation Rule.** Sections alternate between the paper
          + register and the void register down the page. Two adjacent se
          +ctions never share a register unless a real compositional reas
          +on (documented in Components) requires it.                    
      118  
      146 -### 4. Work / Services preview — horizontal scroll            
      119 +**The No-Empty-Frame Rule.** A bordered box, a corner tick mar
          +k, a coordinate caption, or a grid-pattern backdrop is never p
          +laced where a real image is still pending. If the image isn't 
          +built yet, the section waits — it does not ship wearing gift-w
          +rap around an empty box.                                      
      120  
      148 -- **Purpose:** show range and quality without a wall of text; 
          -visitor should feel "they've done this before and at scale" an
          -d want to see more.                                           
      149 -- **Components:** 4–5 curated services as large landscape edit
          -orial tiles in a freely draggable horizontal track (recommende
          -d: AI Voice Agents, Web Development, Digital Marketing, Media 
          -Production, Branding & Identity); each tile uses a full-bleed 
          -dark-washed image as the tile itself (the image IS the surface
          -, not placed inside a card border or rounded box), with servic
          -e name in display type overlaid at bottom-left, category label
          - in small caps at top-left, and a one-line descriptor at the b
          -ottom edge; "View all services →" as the final item in the tra
          -ck (type-only, no tile background); a subtle horizontal drag a
          -ffordance (position indicator — e.g., a thin progress line, no
          -t dots).                                                      
      150 -- **Mobile reflow:** tiles snap-scroll horizontally at approxi
          -mately 85vw width per tile; standard touch momentum scroll; no
          - visible drag cursor.                                         
      151 -- **Mark word:** **AI** — red drawn underline on "AI" in the f
          -irst tile "AI Voice Agents."                                  
      152 -- **Eyebrow:** "Selected Work" is permissible here as this is 
          -section 4 (gap of 3 since Hero). Use it only if the spatial tr
          -ansition from Capabilities needs a heading; omit if the sectio
          -n is self-evident from context. Counts as the second and final
          - eyebrow on the page if used.                                 
      121 +## 3. Typography                                              
      122  
      154 ----                                                           
      123 +**Display Font:** Syne (with sans-serif fallback) — geometric,
          + confident, already the studio's committed identity (not a fre
          +sh pick; identity-preservation applies).                      
      124 +**Body Font:** Manrope (with sans-serif fallback) — a quiet, l
          +egible workhorse that doesn't compete with Syne's geometry.   
      125 +**Label/Mono Font:** JetBrains Mono — eyebrows, captions, tech
          +nical annotations.

      156 -### 5. Testimonial — overlap / collage

          +no marks anything that reads as precise or technical (labels, 
          +captions, real data like coordinates or specs — never decorati
          +ve filler).

          -etrics (2 Wk Avg. Delivery / Avg. Rating / Client Satisfaction
          -) positioned asymmetrically — two metrics at lower-left, one m
          -etric large and offset to the right, intentionally overlapping
          - the quote's bottom edge to create the collage tension; a larg
          -e opening quotation mark in off-white at very low opacity sits
          - behind the text as a structural element, not a decorative flo
          -urish.

          -a 2-column grid then a single row; the overlap is removed on m

      129 +### Hierarchy                                                 
      130 +- **Display** (700, `clamp(2.75rem, 8vw, 7.5rem)`, line-height
          + 0.95): hero and section headlines. One weight, one massive sc
          +ale — the studio's single decisive gesture, not a two-tone spl
          +it.                                                           
      131 +- **Headline** (700, `clamp(2rem, 4vw, 3.5rem)`, line-height 1
          +.0): sub-section statements (e.g. Narrative's problem/solution
          + beats).                                                      
      132 +- **Title** (600, `1.5rem`, line-height 1.2): capability names
          +, work-tile titles.                                           
      133 +- **Body** (400, `1rem`–`1.125rem`, line-height 1.6, max 65ch)
          +: subtitles, descriptions, quotes.                            
      134 +- **Label** (500, `0.6875rem`, line-height 1.4, letter-spacing
          + `0.2em`, uppercase): eyebrows, captions, mono annotations. Us
          +ed sparingly — see the Eyebrow Rule in Components.

      137  
      166 -### 6. CTA / Contact — asymmetric split 60/40                 
      138 +**The Poster Rule.** Hero and section-level headlines commit t
          +o one weight (700) and push scale as far as the viewport allow
          +s (`clamp` ceiling ~7.5rem). No two-weight split-emphasis head
          +lines (e.g. a muted first word next to a bold second word) — t
          +hat reads as timid, not editorial. Emphasis comes from the dra
          +wn red mark, not from a second type weight.

      140 +**The One-Mark Rule.** Exactly one word per section may carry 
          +the drawn red mark (underline, circle, or strike). It is types
          +et in the same weight as the rest of the headline — the mark i
          +tself carries the emphasis, not a font change.

      146 +### Shadow Vocabulary                                         
      147 +- **Hairline** (`0 0 0 1px oklch(30% 0.01 27 / 0.06)`): the on
          +ly border treatment. Used instead of a drop shadow wherever a 
          +boundary needs marking — a panel edge, a hover card.          
      148 +- **Whisper** (`0 1px 2px oklch(20% 0.01 27 / 0.05)`): the onl
          +y permitted drop shadow, for hovering interactive elements onl
          +y (a lifted button, a raised nav bar). Never stacked, never us
          +ed for decoration.

          +w appears only as a response to state (hover, focus) — never a
          +s ambient decoration under a static element.

      156 +### Buttons                                                   
      157 +- **Shape:** fully rounded (`{rounded.full}`), consistent acro
          +ss both registers.                                            
      158 +- **Primary (filled):** `{colors.red-primary}` background, `{c
          +olors.fg}` text, hover → `{colors.red-bright}`. Used where the
          + button is the persistent, always-visible action (Header's "Co
          +nnect").                                                      
      159 +- **Outline:** transparent background, `{colors.red-primary}` 
          +border, ink/fg text depending on register, hover fills to `{co
          +lors.red-primary}` with fg text. Used where the button is a fi
          +rst, calmer impression rather than a persistent control (Hero'
          +s "Start a project").                                         
      160 +- **Voice split is deliberate, not drift:** persistent nav CTA
          + = filled (urgency, always visible); in-page hero/section CTA 
          += outline (refined, first impression). Both registers share th
          +is logic; do not force one voice everywhere.

      163 +Fixed overlay, present on every route. Logo (`public/rs logo.p
          +ng` — white wordmark + red-script "Studio"; no dark-ink varian
          +t exists yet, see Do's and Don'ts), nav links (Services · Proc
          +ess · Capabilities · Work · Contact), plain status text "Avail
          +able for projects" (no pill, no badge), "Connect" button (fill
          +ed primary, small, rightmost). Because the logo is white-only 
          +and the bar now floats over both light and dark sections, the 
          +bar is a **permanent dark instrument surface** (not transparen
          +t-until-scroll) so the logo stays legible everywhere. Mobile:

          +inned at drawer bottom. Structural only — the drawn-mark motif
          + and eyebrows never appear here.

          +aceholder frame standing in for it; until the real asset exist
          +s, the image zone is an honest solid/gradient crimson field (a

          +es the positioning on its own (dropping the previous "Digital

      190  
      236 -### Resolved violations (from previous audit)                 
      237 -All 9 items from the 2026-06-30 audit have been resolved:     
      238 -1. No `window.addEventListener("scroll")` — all IntersectionOb

      239 -2. No scroll cue — removed

          -ccentGlow

      191 +### Do:                                                       
      192 +- **Do** treat red as a material in light sections — a real ma
          +ss (field, object, filled button), never a hairline.          
      193 +- **Do** let a whole section's background be a real image when
          + the content calls for it (Work preview tiles, Hero's image zo
          +ne) — small boxed illustration slots are not an acceptable sub
          +stitute for the real subject.                                 
      194 +- **Do** give every section exactly one dominant, load-bearing
          + gesture.                                                     
      195 +- **Do** use the drawn red mark (underline / circle / strike) 
          +exactly once per section, varying the gesture so the motif doe
          +sn't feel mechanical.                                         
      196 +- **Do** alternate light (paper) and dark (void) registers sec
          +tion to section, tied together by the one red hue.            
      197 +- **Do** use `{rounded.full}` buttons consistently: filled for
          + persistent nav actions, outline for first-impression in-page 
          +CTAs.                                                         
      198 +- **Do** keep neutrals tinted toward the red hue at low chroma
          + in both registers — never a true 0-chroma gray.

      200 +### Don't:                                                    
      201 +- **Don't** use purple/blue SaaS gradients, glassmorphism, or 
          +generic "AI startup" gradients anywhere.                      
      202 +- **Don't** use Inter, Roboto, Space Grotesk, or Open Sans as 
          +defaults — Syne / Manrope / JetBrains Mono are locked.        
      203 +- **Don't** ship a placeholder frame (dashed border, corner re
          +gistration ticks, coordinate caption, grid-pattern backdrop) s
          +tanding in for a real image that hasn't been built yet. Wait f
          +or the real asset, or fill the space with an honest solid/grad
          +ient crimson field instead.                                   
      204 +- **Don't** assemble a hero (or any section) from many small t
          +imid elements — a rail, an eyebrow, a two-tone headline, an ar
          +row glyph, a subtitle, and a button all competing quietly. One
          + dominant gesture per section.                                
      205 +- **Don't** use a floating rounded glass-pill nav with backdro
          +p blur and decorative corner flourishes — the most recognizabl
          +e 2024-25 AI nav shape. Header's permanent instrument-bar solv
          +es the real legibility problem (white-only logo) without reach
          +ing for that shape.                                           
      206 +- **Don't** repeat a layout family on two adjacent sections.  
      207 +- **Don't** add more than one eyebrow per three sections; most
          + sections carry none.                                         
      208 +- **Don't** let "Start a project" / "Connect" appear more than
          + three times on the landing page (Header, Hero, Contact).     
      209 +- **Don't** introduce a second accent hue for any reason witho
          +ut an explicit, documented exception.

⏺ Both files are rewritten. Summary of the actual changes, not just the format
  switch:

  DESIGN.md is now genuinely different in substance, not just reformatted:
  - Red is now a material, not an accent — the biggest fix. Named Rules (The 
  Material Rule) now explicitly require red to occupy real visible mass in light
  sections (a field, a filled object), never a hairline underline standing in
  for the whole brand.
  - The "no empty frame" rule (The No-Empty-Frame Rule) makes explicit what went
  wrong before: placeholder chrome (dashed borders, coordinate captions, corner
  ticks) is banned from standing in for content that isn't built yet. If the
  image isn't ready, the section either waits or gets an honest solid crimson
  field — never decorated emptiness.
  - "Whole background can be an image" is now written in directly — Work
  preview's five tiles and Hero's image zone are specified as full-bleed, not
  boxed illustration slots, matching what you said today.
  - The old bespoke "Landing — Architecture" doc (which wasn't in any format any
  Impeccable command could actually read) is folded into the Components
  section, where the spec has room for it — so it survives, but now something
  following Impeccable's rules can act on it.
  - Full color/typography tokens with real hex values (converted from the OKLCH
  tokens already in globals.css) and named colors, per the actual spec format.

  PRODUCT.md — tightened Brand Personality and Anti-references to name the
  specific failures (decorative-chrome-as-content, the generic floating-pill
  nav, assembled-from-parts heroes) instead of leaving them only in conversation
  history.

  I haven't touched any component code this turn — this was the foundation pass
  you asked for. Want to review these files yourself first, or should I move
  straight into rebuilding Hero against this corrected spec?

      114  
      115  **The Material Rule.** In light sections, red is never a hairl
           ine. If red appears, it occupies a real, visible mass — a satu
           rated field, a bleeding object, a filled button — never a 1px 
           underline doing all the work of representing the brand. Hairli
           ne red is a dark-section move (the drawn mark); light sections
            commit.
      116

          - register and the void register down the page. Two adjacent se
          -ctions never share a register unless a real compositional reas
          -on (documented in Components) requires it.

      119  **The No-Empty-Frame Rule.** A bordered box, a corner tick mar

⏺ Clean. Composition now genuinely matches the reference language:
  full-viewport, image-integrated bottom-anchored typography, minimal chrome, no
  gradient masquerading as content.

  The image brief — this is the one thing standing between this and the real
  thing, so here's a properly specific, non-generic prompt (named lighting,
  named material, named mood — not "clean modern tech"):

  ▎ A single monumental abstract sculptural form, humanoid-adjacent but not 
  ▎ literally a figure — molten obsidian-black material with a single visible 
  ▎ seam of brilliant crimson light bleeding through a crack in its surface, as 
  ▎ if something incandescent is contained just beneath. Camera looks up 
  ▎ slightly, low angle, so the form dominates and recedes into darkness at the 
  ▎ edges of frame. Lighting: single hard key light from upper-left raking 
  ▎ across the material to reveal texture and fracture detail, everything else 
  ▎ falls to near-black — no fill light softening the shadows. Atmosphere: fine 
  ▎ floating dust or smoke catching the key light, subtle film grain throughout,
  ▎ shot on a dark warm-black background (not pure black, not blue-black). 
  ▎ Composition: subject occupies the upper two-thirds of frame, off-center to 
  ▎ the right, leaving the lower-left third in near-total shadow for text to sit
  ▎ against. No readable face, no literal human anatomy, no floating debris 
  ▎ clichés, no symmetric framing, no blue-tinted rim light. 
  ▎ Portrait/vertical-leaning crop, roughly 4:5 to 3:4. Reference mood: a 
  ▎ museum-lit sculpture crossed with a film teaser poster — quiet, massive, a 
  ▎ little ominous, unmistakably expensive.

  Want me to move on to Header (make it match this darker, more cinematic
  register properly instead of just "permanent dark bar as a fix"), or hold here
  so you can react to Hero first?

  ▎ machine awake alone at 3am.

  ▎ and down over the city, catching faint atmospheric haze as it travels,

  ▎ further than you can see.

  ▎ gesture — an underline, a circle, a decisive stroke — and the camera caught 
  ▎ the entire motion as one unbroken glowing line. The light-trail has genuine 
  ▎ long-exposure qualities: soft-edged, brightest at its core with a faint red 
  ▎ bloom around it, subtle variation in brightness along its length where the 
  ▎ movement sped up or slowed — not a perfectly uniform CG line. Everything 
  ▎ else is true black: no visible walls, floor, or furniture, just the gesture 
  ▎ itself suspended in darkness. At one end, where the stroke began, the 
  ▎ faintest ghosted suggestion of a figure or hand — motion-blurred almost past
  ▎ recognition, more implication than form. Fine dust catches stray light near
  ▎ the brightest point of the stroke. Composition: the light-stroke sweeps 
  ▎ from the upper right down toward the lower-center, leaving the lower-left 
  ▎ third of the frame genuinely empty and dark. Landscape 16:9, full-bleed, 
  ▎ authentic photographic grain, no digital glow/render sheen. Mood: a single 
  ▎ decisive mark, caught in the instant it was made.

          +ning through both                                             
       85 +- One dominant, load-bearing gesture per section — never an as
          +sembly of small decorated parts                               
       86 +- Real imagery fills whole sections when the brief calls for i
          +t; imagery is never boxed into a small illustration slot stand
          +ing in for the real subject                                   
       87 +- The drawn red "mark" (underline, circle, strike) is the recu
          +rring brand signature — one per section, never more

       91 +The palette is genuinely one hue (red, ~27° in OKLCH) expresse
          +d as two neutral registers — a warm paper/ink pair for light s
          +ections, a near-black/off-white pair for dark sections — plus 
          +a six-step red ramp shared by both. Neutrals in both registers
          + are tinted a few thousandths of chroma toward the red hue; th
          +ere is no true 0-chroma gray anywhere in the system.

          -page.

          -; fake `<div>`

       93 +### Primary                                                   
       94 +- **Foundry Crimson** (`#b91319` / `oklch(0.5 0.195 27)`): the
          + one accent. Used as a dominant material in light sections (a 
          +saturated field, a hero object) and as a precise mark in dark 
          +sections (underline, CTA fill, focus ring). Never a second hue
          + anywhere.                                                    
       95 +- **Foundry Bright** (`#ac0000` / `oklch(0.445 0.215 26)`): ho
          +ver/active state for the primary red — brighter, not lighter. 
       96 +- **Foundry Deep / Oxblood** (`#5f000b`, `#2c0005`): the dark 
          +end of the ramp — shadow-side of the material, deep accents on
          + dark surfaces.                                               
       97 +- **Dusky Rose / Brick** (`#f6d9d9`, `#e58b8c`): the light end
          + of the ramp — tints, washes, the material seen at its coolest

          + Never pure white — tinted toward the red hue at very low chro
          +ma.

          +s, cards.

      105 +### Neutral — Dark register (Void)

      112  
       91 -## Landing — Architecture                                     
      113 +**The One Hue Rule.** Every color in this system shares one hu
          +e family. If a second hue is ever needed (a semantic error/suc
          +cess state, for instance), it must be proposed and approved ex
          +plicitly — it does not default in.                            
      114  
       93 -> Binding constraints carried through: dark single-mode, one d
          -eep-red drawn mark                                            
       94 -> per section (animates on scroll-reveal), one CTA intent, VAR
          -IANCE 6 / MOTION 5 /                                          
       95 -> DENSITY 3, max 1 eyebrow per 3 sections, no scroll cues, no 
          -decorative dots,                                              
       96 -> no 3 equal-width cards, no images inside card containers.   
      115 +**The Material Rule.** In light sections, red is never a hairl
          +ine. If red appears, it occupies a real, visible mass — a satu
          +rated field, a bleeding object, a filled button — never a 1px 
          +underline doing all the work of representing the brand. Hairli
          +ne red is a dark-section move (the drawn mark); light sections
          + commit.

          - a project".

          -ill, three placements only).                                  
      101 -"Connect" in the nav is the same intent on a different surface
          - — same red fill, smaller.                                    
      117 +**The Alternation Rule.** Sections alternate between the paper
          + register and the void register down the page. Two adjacent se
          +ctions never share a register unless a real compositional reas
          +on (documented in Components) requires it. *(Revised 2026-07-0
          +1: Hero itself is dark/void — see Components — reopening which
          + section anchors the alternation. Narrative, immediately after
          + Hero, should move to paper when it gets its own pass, so the 
          +two sections adjacent to each other still alternate.)*

      103 ----                                                           
      119 +**The No-Empty-Frame Rule.** A bordered box, a corner tick mar
          +k, a coordinate caption, or a grid-pattern backdrop is never p
          +laced where a real image is still pending. If the image isn't 
          +built yet, the section waits — it does not ship wearing gift-w
          +rap around an empty box.

      115 -### 1. Hero — full-bleed hero                                 
      129 +### Hierarchy                                                 
      130 +- **Display** (700, `clamp(2.75rem, 8vw, 7.5rem)`, line-height
          + 0.95): hero and section headlines. One weight, one massive sc
          +ale — the studio's single decisive gesture, not a two-tone spl
          +it.                                                           
      131 +- **Headline** (700, `clamp(2rem, 4vw, 3.5rem)`, line-height 1
          +.0): sub-section statements (e.g. Narrative's problem/solution
          + beats).                                                      
      132 +- **Title** (600, `1.5rem`, line-height 1.2): capability names
          +, work-tile titles.                                           
      133 +- **Body** (400, `1rem`–`1.125rem`, line-height 1.6, max 65ch)
          +: subtitles, descriptions, quotes.                            
      134 +- **Label** (500, `0.6875rem`, line-height 1.4, letter-spacing
          + `0.2em`, uppercase): eyebrows, captions, mono annotations. Us
          +ed sparingly — see the Eyebrow Rule in Components.            
      135  
      117 -- **Purpose:** land the brand proposition in one breath; visit
          -or should feel "this is serious and different from every agenc
          -y site."                                                      
      118 -- **Components:** eyebrow "Digital Solutions Agency" (this sec
          -tion's single allocated eyebrow — counts as 1 of max 1-per-3),
          - split display headline **IDEAS → REALITY** (two typographic w
          -eights, the arrow as a motion beat), subtitle line "intelligen
          -t digital solutions" (subdued scale, generous tracking), auto-
          -scrolling trust ticker strip (Web Development · Brand Identity
          - · Conversational AI · Creative Production · Digital Marketing
          - — horizontal marquee, not a static row of pills), stats row (
          -Happy Clients / Projects Delivered / Support Available — three
          - raw numbers with labels, sparse horizontal layout, not inside
          - cards or equal boxes), "Start a project" CTA (red fill, the p
          -age's primary button).                                        
      119 -- **Full-bleed treatment:** near-black canvas; either a dark-w
          -ashed cinematic still (architectural, city, or abstract — no f
          -aces, no generic stock) or a pure near-black textured surface.
          - The image is the background, never boxed.                    
      120 -- **Mobile reflow:** headline wraps to two lines; trust ticker
          - continues auto-scrolling at same speed; stats collapse to a 2
          -+1 arrangement; CTA stretches full-width.                     
      121 -- **Mark word:** **REALITY** — red drawn underline animates in
          - under "REALITY" on initial load (this is the one instance whe
          -re the mark appears on load rather than scroll-reveal, as it a
          -nchors the concept immediately).                              
      136 +### Named Rules                                               
      137  
      123 ----                                                           
      138 +**The Poster Rule.** Hero and section-level headlines commit t
          +o one weight (700) and push scale as far as the viewport allow
          +s (`clamp` ceiling ~7.5rem). No two-weight split-emphasis head
          +lines (e.g. a muted first word next to a bold second word) — t
          +hat reads as timid, not editorial. Emphasis comes from the dra
          +wn red mark, not from a second type weight.

      125 -### 2. Narrative — sticky-scroll narrative                    
      140 +**The One-Mark Rule.** Exactly one word per section may carry 
          +the drawn red mark (underline, circle, or strike). It is types
          +et in the same weight as the rest of the headline — the mark i
          +tself carries the emphasis, not a font change.

      146 +### Shadow Vocabulary                                         
      147 +- **Hairline** (`0 0 0 1px oklch(30% 0.01 27 / 0.06)`): the on
          +ly border treatment. Used instead of a drop shadow wherever a 
          +boundary needs marking — a panel edge, a hover card.          
      148 +- **Whisper** (`0 1px 2px oklch(20% 0.01 27 / 0.05)`): the onl
          +y permitted drop shadow, for hovering interactive elements onl
          +y (a lifted button, a raised nav bar). Never stacked, never us
          +ed for decoration.                                            
      149

          -uilt — visitor thinks "they do this specifically, not generica
          -lly."

          -(01 through 06); each row contains: index number (small, muted
          -, left-aligned), capability name in large display type (center
          - or left), brief one-line descriptor either right-aligned on t
          -he same row or revealed below on interaction; a full-width hai
          -rline rule divides each row; rows expand inline on hover/focus
          - to reveal the full descriptor — no separate cards, no equal-w
          -idth columns, no bounding boxes; row height is intentionally u
          -nequal (some capabilities have longer names, creating natural 
          -visual rhythm).

      193 +## 6. Do's and Don'ts                                         
      194  
      198 -**Distinct layout families used: 7** (requirement: ≥ 4). No tw
          -o adjacent content sections share a family.                   
      195 +### Do:                                                       
      196 +- **Do** treat red as a material in light sections — a real ma
          +ss (field, object, filled button), never a hairline.          
      197 +- **Do** let a whole section's background be a real image when
          + the content calls for it (Work preview tiles, Hero's image zo
          +ne) — small boxed illustration slots are not an acceptable sub
          +stitute for the real subject.                                 
      198 +- **Do** give every section exactly one dominant, load-bearing
          + gesture.                                                     
      199 +- **Do** use the drawn red mark (underline / circle / strike) 
          +exactly once per section, varying the gesture so the motif doe
          +sn't feel mechanical.                                         
      200 +- **Do** alternate light (paper) and dark (void) registers sec
          +tion to section, tied together by the one red hue.            
      201 +- **Do** use `{rounded.full}` buttons consistently: filled for
          + persistent nav actions, outline for first-impression in-page 
          +CTAs.                                                         
      202 +- **Do** keep neutrals tinted toward the red hue at low chroma
          + in both registers — never a true 0-chroma gray.              
      203 +- **Do** ground every image concept in what Remark actually is
          + (a digital/tech agency) and in the studio's own craft, not a 
          +borrowed aesthetic from a reference site — extract the *techni
          +que* (depth, lighting, composition), never the subject matter 
          +wholesale.                                                    
      204  
      200 ----                                                           
      205 +### Don't:                                                    
      206 +- **Don't** use purple/blue SaaS gradients, glassmorphism, or 
          +generic "AI startup" gradients anywhere.                      
      207 +- **Don't** use Inter, Roboto, Space Grotesk, or Open Sans as 
          +defaults — Syne / Manrope / JetBrains Mono are locked.        
      208 +- **Don't** ship a placeholder frame (dashed border, corner re
          +gistration ticks, coordinate caption, grid-pattern backdrop) s
          +tanding in for a real image that hasn't been built yet. Wait f
          +or the real asset, or fill the space with an honest solid/grad
          +ient crimson field instead.                                   
      209 +- **Don't** assemble a hero (or any section) from many small t
          +imid elements — a rail, an eyebrow, a two-tone headline, an ar
          +row glyph, a subtitle, and a button all competing quietly. One
          + dominant gesture per section.                                
      210 +- **Don't** use a floating rounded glass-pill nav with backdro
          +p blur and decorative corner flourishes — the most recognizabl
          +e 2024-25 AI nav shape. Header's flush treatment solves the re
          +al legibility problem (white-only logo, dark-by-default sectio
          +ns) without reaching for that shape.                          
      211 +- **Don't** repeat a layout family on two adjacent sections.  
      212 +- **Don't** add more than one eyebrow per three sections; most
          + sections carry none.                                         
      213 +- **Don't** let "Start a project" / "Connect" appear more than
          + three times on the landing page (Header, Hero, Contact).     
      214 +- **Don't** introduce a second accent hue for any reason witho
          +ut an explicit, documented exception.                         
      215 +- **Don't** let hero/tile imagery read as isolated product pho
          +tography (a single dramatic object on a void background) — it 
          +must feel like the studio's actual work or craft, not a trophy
          + shot.                                                        
      216  
      202 -### Repetition risks — flagged for downstream agents          
      203 -                                                              
      204 -**RISK 1 — Mobile reflow similarity (sections 2 and 3).**     
      205 -Sticky-scroll narrative and stacked index list both reflow to 
          -full-width vertical stacks on mobile. They are visually distin
          -ct on desktop (split sticky panel vs. ruled rows) but the `lay
          -out-spacing-engineer` must enforce a clear visual break betwee
          -n them on small screens — different type scale, different back
          -ground lightness treatment (section 2 slightly lighter or sect
          -ion 3 with a full-width rule to open it), or a section-gap lar
          -ge enough that the transition reads as intentional.           
      206 -                                                              
      207 -**RISK 2 — Eyebrow budget.**                                  
      208 -Hero (section 1) uses the only mandatory eyebrow. Work preview
          - (section 4) may use a second one ("Selected Work") — that is 
          -2 eyebrows across 7 content sections (ratio: 1-per-3.5). Withi
          -n budget. No other section should receive an eyebrow under any
          - circumstance.                                                
      209 -                                                              
      210 -**RISK 3 — CTA over-recurrence.**                             
      211 -"Start a project" (red fill) appears in: Header ("Connect" equ
          -ivalent), Hero, and CTA/Contact. Any additional placement — su
          -ch as a "get started" link at the end of Capabilities or Work 
          -preview — would violate the one-loud-element rule and must be 
          -rejected by the `design-qa-auditor`. Secondary text links ("Se
          -e all services →") are acceptable and do not count as CTA recu
          -rrences.                                                      
      212 -                                                              
      213 -**RISK 4 — Mark gesture monotony.**                           
      214 -Seven sections means seven drawn marks. The `motion-designer` 
          -must vary the mark gesture across sections to prevent the moti
          -f from feeling mechanical: underline (Hero), underline (Narrat
          -ive), circle (Capabilities), underline (Work), underline (Test
          -imonial), underline (CTA) — that is five underlines and one ci
          -rcle. The motion specialist should introduce at least one addi
          -tional gesture variant (strike, bracket, or partial arc) to ma
          -intain the "hand-drawn editor" feeling across the full scroll.

⏺ Clean. What actually changed this pass (not just color):

  - Typography: problem statements bumped from a timid 2.8rem ceiling to 4rem,
  and the payoff line to 5rem — both now carry real weight instead of reading as
  body copy with a bigger font-size.
  - Motion: reveal now blurs into focus (blur-[2px] → blur-none) alongside
  fade/translate, instead of a generic opacity+slide — a genuine "focus pull,"
  not the default scroll-reveal everyone ships.
  - Micro-interaction: passed (dimmed) problem statements now brighten on hover
  — a small, real, functional touch, not decoration.
  - A real ordinal: "01 / 03" tied to actual scroll state — earns its place
  because it's a genuine 3-item sequence, unlike a decorative section number.
  - Visual weight on the payoff: a soft red glow behind "scalable digital
  solutions," tying back to the "red as material" language instead of leaving
  the mark as a lone underline doing all the work.

⏺ Clean, no errors. Summary of the audit:

  Fixed:
  - Narrative's progress-dot was animating top (layout-triggering, forces reflow
  every frame) — switched to transform: translateY(), GPU-composited.
  - Narrative's mb-32 gap between problem statements was a size bump I made
  without real justification — pulled back to mb-24.
  - Header's nav-link underline was growing via width with transition-all (a
  named anti-pattern — animates properties indiscriminately, including ones that
  should stay instant) — switched to scale-x transform with an explicit
  transition-transform.
  - Confirmed the focus ring isn't caught in any transition (it's
  box-shadow-based via ring-*, and none of the nearby
  transition-colors/transition-transform declarations touch it) — so it still
  appears instantly on keyboard focus, as required.

  Verified clean: Hero's animations (animate-fade-in-up keyframe, hover
  transforms) were already transform/opacity-only. Global prefers-reduced-motion
  override in globals.css catches all of this automatically since it forces
  transition-duration/animation-duration to near-zero universally.

  No visual regressions — type-check and console both clean. Want to move to
  Capabilities next, or keep auditing?

⏺ Clean. Summary of everything found and fixed in this pass:

  Real bugs fixed:
  1. Bottom gap — min-h-[200vh] was far more scroll height than the content
  needed, leaving ~35% of it as dead space after the payoff line settled, before
  Capabilities began. Reduced to min-h-[150vh]; the transition now happens
  right as the text finishes appearing.
  2. Phase/label desync — the "Problem"/"Solution" label was inferring phase
  from a guessed scroll percentage, which didn't match where the actual solution
  text sat in the document (it isn't sticky, so its position isn't proportional
  to scroll fraction). Replaced the guess with a direct measurement: a ref on
  the actual solution block, checked against real viewport position. Verified
  live via DOM query, not just a screenshot.
  3. top animation (progress dot) — was animating a layout-triggering property;
  switched to transform: translateY() so the browser composites it on the GPU
  instead of relayouting every frame.
  4. transition-all + width-based underline (Header nav hover) — a named
  anti-pattern; switched to transform: scaleX() with an explicit
  transition-transform.
  5. Unjustified mb-32 — I'd bumped spacing between problem statements without
  real reason; pulled back to mb-24.

  Which image, for what: Narrative doesn't need one — per its own spec it's
  intentionally pure typography (sticky-scroll editorial, no imagery called
  for), and that's still correct after this pass. Same for Capabilities (stacked
  index list, text-only). The actual open image need is the Work preview 
  section — 4 of its 5 tile images are still pending (only the AI Voice Agents
  one shipped). Want me to hand you the next one now (Web Development), or
  finish Capabilities first per the one-section-at-a-time order?

⏺ No errors, clean. Both SVG elements now animate in genuine response to scroll
  rather than sitting static:

  What changed:
  - EdgeGeometry gained an optional progress prop — its corner lines draw
  themselves in via stroke-dashoffset, staggered one after another rather than
  all at once. Fully backward-compatible (every other usage across the site —
  Contact, About, Work, Footer — is unaffected since it's opt-in).
  - AccentGlow gained an optional progress prop — a subtle translate3d parallax
  drift as you scroll through the section, GPU-composited, no layout cost.
  - Wired both into Narrative: the corner geometry now draws in exactly as the
  "good enough → scalable" solution beat arrives (tied to the same real
  DOM-position read that already drives the phase label, so it can't desync),
  and the glow drifts slowly across the full section scroll.

  Verified live: real scroll input fires the IntersectionObserver correctly
  (confirmed via console — ratio 0.27 → 0.60 as expected), the lines draw
  progressively (screenshots at two scroll depths show 2-of-4 then all 4 lines
  present), and no console errors.

  One caveat worth flagging: while testing I found that programmatic
  window.scrollTo() via devtools doesn't reliably trigger this
  IntersectionObserver in this environment — only real scroll input does. That's
  a quirk of the automation tooling, not a bug in the site (confirmed the app
  itself is correct via real scroll), but worth knowing if you ever script-test
  scroll behavior here again.

⏺ Caught a real scroll-mechanics bug: the corner geometry is pinned to the
  section's top edge, but its reveal was gated on 1/3 of the entire section's
  scroll (rows + marquee), so by the time it's supposed to draw in, its anchor
  point has already scrolled off the top of the screen. Fixing with the same
  lesson from Narrative — tie the reveal to actual scrolled pixels near the top,
  not a fraction of total section height.

⏺ Found it — a real, confirmed bug in the code, not a screenshot artifact this
  time. The progress track and progress dot both combine a transition-transform 
  duration-500 CSS transition with a value that's being updated on nearly every
  scroll tick. That's a direct conflict: each new scroll-driven update restarts
  a fresh 500ms easing from wherever the previous one had gotten to, so the dot
  perpetually lags and "catches up" late instead of tracking the scroll 1:1 — a
  classic scroll-scrubbing bug. Let me confirm by reading the relevant code once
  more.

⏺ Now checking a mobile-specific issue I'd reasoned through earlier from the
  code but hadn't confirmed: the "sticky left label" only gets md:sticky — below
  that breakpoint it's a plain block. On a 1-column grid, CSS auto-placement
  puts the label and the content in separate grid rows, so even adding sticky
  unconditionally wouldn't help — the label's own row is short, leaving it
  nothing to stick against. That means on phones, the "Problem/Solution" tracker
  never actually tracks; it scrolls away in the first second and users never
  see it update.

⏺ Clean. Here's what I found on this deeper pass through Narrative — two real
  bugs, both confirmed via code/DOM, not just visual guessing (screenshots kept
  lying to me at small sizes, so I verified everything through computed styles):

  Bug 1 — progress dot/track fighting the scroll (fixed): The vertical progress
  line and its lit dot both had transition-transform duration-500 while also
  being driven by scroll position on nearly every tick. That's a direct conflict
  — each scroll update would restart a fresh 500ms ease from wherever the dot
  had gotten to, so it perpetually lagged and rubber-banded instead of tracking
  your scroll 1:1. This is almost certainly what read as "scroll animation
  mistakes" — it's the most visible, constantly-moving element in the section.
  Removed the transitions; confirmed via computed style that transitionDuration
  is now 0s and the transform snaps to the exact scroll-derived value.

  Bug 2 — sticky label silently broken on mobile (fixed): Below the md:
  breakpoint, the sticky column had no room to actually stick — CSS Grid's
  single-column auto-placement puts the label and content in separate rows, so
  the label's own row was short and sticky had nothing tall to work within. In
  practice this meant the entire "Problem → Solution" tracker — the section's
  whole mechanic — just scrolled away in the first second on any phone and never
  updated again. Fixed by switching mobile to flex-col (so label and content
  share one tall flex parent, giving sticky real room) while keeping the desktop
  grid untouched. Verified by forcing the collapsed layout at the current
  viewport and confirming the label now stays pinned while content scrolls past
  it.

  I checked Capabilities and WorkPreview for the same transition-vs-scroll
  conflict — neither has it, this was isolated to Narrative's progress
  indicator.

  One caveat: this environment's window-resize tool isn't producing a true
  narrow viewport (stuck at the physical display's 1680px regardless of what I
  request), so I verified the sticky fix by forcing the mobile CSS state at
  desktop width rather than a real phone screen. The mechanism is solid, but
  worth a real spot-check on an actual phone when you get a chance.

  ⎿  Added 37 lines
       1  "use client";
       2  
       3 +import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";           
       4 +                                                               
       5 +/** A vertical red thread crossing a section boundary — half in
         + the section above,                                            
       6 +    half in the one below — drawn downward on scroll. The brand
         +'s mark used                                                   
       7 +    structurally: it stitches two registers together so a seam 
         +reads as sewn,                                                 
       8 +    not cut. Place as a zero-height sibling BETWEEN two section
         +s (page level),                                                
       9 +    never inside one — sections with overflow-hidden would clip
         + its upper half. */                                            
      10 +export function SeamStitch({ className = "" }: { className?: st
         +ring }) {                                                      
      11 +  const ref = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);                    
      12 +  const [visible, setVisible] = useState(false);               
      13 +                                                               
      14 +  useEffect(() => {                                            
      15 +    const el = ref.current;                                    
      16 +    if (!el) return;                                           
      17 +    const io = new IntersectionObserver(                       
      18 +      ([entry]) => {                                           
      19 +        if (entry.isIntersecting) {                            
      20 +          setVisible(true);                                    
      21 +          io.disconnect();                                     
      22 +        }                                                      
      23 +      },                                                       
      24 +      { rootMargin: "-10% 0px" }                               
      25 +    );                                                         
      26 +    io.observe(el);                                            
      27 +    return () => io.disconnect();                              
      28 +  }, []);                                                      
      29 +                                                               
      30 +  return (                                                     
      31 +    <div ref={ref} aria-hidden="true" className={`relative z-10
         + h-0 ${className}`}>                                           
      32 +      <span                                                    
      33 +        className="absolute left-1/2 top-0 -ml-px block h-24 w-
         +0.5 origin-top bg-accent transition-transform duration-700 ease
         +-out-expo md:h-32"                                             
      34 +        style={{ transform: `translateY(-50%) scaleY(${visible 
         +? 1 : 0})` }}                                                  
      35 +      />                                                       
      36 +    </div>                                                     
      37 +  );                                                           
      38 +}                                                              
      39 +

      41    className = "",
      42    size = 60,

⏺ Done — the division feel had four root causes, all fixed and verified live end
  to end:

  What was making it feel like stacked bands:
  1. Full-width hairline borders at every seam (TrustStrip border-y, StatsBar
  border-y) — literal drawn lines announcing "section over." All removed.
  2. StatsBar was a pure divider band — the centered 150+/300+/24-7 trio is the
  exact "hero-metric template" your skills ban, and it duplicated Testimonial's
  own three metrics (100%, 2Wk, 4.9). Six big numbers in three sections was
  metric soup. Deleted from the page (component file kept — one line restores it
  if you want it back).
  3. Nothing ever crossed a boundary. New SeamStitch: a short crimson thread
  that sits half in each section and draws itself downward on scroll — your
  "mark" used structurally to sew the register changes together (dark→paper,
  paper→dark, testimonial→contact). Verified drawing at all three seams in the
  browser.
  4. Uniform padding = uniform bands. TrustStrip now sits on the hero's void as
  its closing caption; Testimonial→Contact padding is compressed so proof flows
  straight into "Ready to scale" as one movement.

  Plus small polish from make-interfaces-feel-better: antialiased on body,
  overflow-x: clip safety net, Contact's CTA fixed from rounded to the spec's
  rounded-full, fake hover removed from non-interactive TrustStrip text.

  image-prompts.txt is at the project root — it locks the visual world, lists
  the 6 already-shipped images so they don't get regenerated, marks
  Narrative/Capabilities as intentionally imageless, and gives full prompts for
  the 3 remaining: Contact closer (workshop door ajar, red light spilling out),
  About-page hero, and an optional testimonial backdrop.

  Two flags: the design hook keeps reporting 3 pre-existing globals.css values
  (--color-border white-alpha, scrollbar 3px radius) that predate today — say
  the word and I'll either tokenize them in DESIGN.md or add the ignore. And
  nothing from the last two days is committed to git yet — after the
  external-revert incident, I'd strongly suggest letting me commit now.
